Instead of continuous density, we can directly work with sequences and asymptotics of P-like functions.

Let's think of exact sequences that make A(z)^2 coefficients nearly constant 1. The infinite P sequence has A∞(z)= (1-z)^(-1/2) (up to scale) whose square is 1/(1-z), coefficients all 1. Truncating at n causes tail deficits. To improve, perhaps choose a finite sequence whose square approximates 1/(1-z) on first n and also on last n by using a sequence that is a finite approximate square root of the finite geometric series 1+...+z^{2n-2}. The exact coefficientwise square root maybe can be computed as the first n coefficients of sqrt{(1-z^{2n-1})/(1-z)}. The first n coefficients are just P_i (since z^{2n-1} term only affects coefficients ≥2n-1, none in first n). Thus the maximal sequence satisfying c_k≤1 for k<n is P truncated. But we can use tail slack to increase last coefficients, at cost of reducing some early constraints. This is like finding a finite polynomial A of degree n-1 maximizing A(1) subject to A(z)^2 ≤ G(z)=1+...+z^{2n-2} coefficientwise. This is a finite coefficientwise square root problem. There may be an exact solution via **Taylor square root with truncation and rounding**. The coefficientwise maximum square root of G may not be the principal Taylor root because finite degree constraints at high coefficients matter. There is theory: given a formal power series G with positive coefficients, the maximal nonnegative coefficient sequence whose square ≤G can be computed by a recursive congruence? Since support finite, we need A^2 coefficient ≤G. The Taylor square root P satisfies the first n coefficients exactly 1 and tail <1. To use tail slack, we can consider increasing A's high-degree coefficients. Let A = P_N + E, where E has zeros for low degrees perhaps. The change in c_k = 2(P*E)_k + E*E_k. For low k, P*E may be positive; to keep ≤1, E must be negative? But E coefficients can be positive at high degrees; for low k < degree(E), (P*E)_k=0. So if E starts at degree m>0, it won't affect early k<m. It will affect mid/tail. Thus we can add a high-degree block that is itself a square root of the leftover slack in tail constraints, without affecting early constraints up to its starting degree. This suggests a recursive construction: The sequence can be a sum/superposition of P sequences shifted to the right, using slack in later coefficients. That's like representing G as a sum of finitely many scaled shifted square roots? Each block anchored at a starting position uses capacity in its local window. The optimal may be greedy: allocate mass as a reflected P starting at each position to fill slack. This resembles coding / pulse shaping.

In other words, constraints c_k≤1, to maximize sum, we can view each possible coefficient a_i as creating a "profile" of convolution contributions. The P sequence from i=0 is the maximal impulse response. If we delay a P pulse to start at m, it uses coefficients m..2m+n? and has sum ~2√n. We can pack such pulses? But support length fixed n, so pulses can overlap. This is too complex.

Maybe the optimal is given by the **Hadamard square root of the all-ones vector** truncated? Coordinate ascent can get stuck but monotonicity helps.

We can implement **coordinate descent with occasional decreases**: For fixed cap M, to improve shape, we might reduce a coordinate that is active bottleneck and increase others. Since cap T is current max, we can do:
- Start with a normalized from best.
- For many iterations:
   1. Perturb: multiply a by (1+noise) on random subset, or add small Gaussian, clip, normalize.
   2. Set cap T = max(conv(a)) * (1 + slack) maybe (or target cap).
   3. Run coordinate max passes to maximize sum under T until convergence or time.
   4. Score; if better, update.
This is a constructive search. Coordinate max is fast; perturbations many.

Coordinate max cap functions:
```
def _coord_cap_optimize(a, cap, max_passes, tol=1e-12, verbose=False):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a[a<0]=0
    n = len(a)
    s0 = a.sum()
    if s0 <=0: return a, np.inf
    # Ensure max(c) <= cap * (1+1e-10)
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    maxc = float(c.max())
    if maxc > cap:
        # scale down a to satisfy cap? Since c scales with square, factor sqrt(cap/maxc)
        a *= np.sqrt(cap / maxc)
        c = _fft_convolve(a)
        maxc = float(c.max())
    # If maxc < cap and cap too large, okay. We can use cap absolute.
    prev_s = a.sum()
    for pass in range(max_passes):
        # maybe randomize order to avoid direction bias
        order = np.random.permutation(n) if n<=2000 else np.arange(n)
        changed_any=False
        total_new=prev_s
        for i in order:
            old = a[i]
            # compute limit vectorized
            # seg = c[i:i+n]
            # base = seg - 2*old*a
            # denom = 2*a
            # limits = (cap - base)/denom for denom>0
            # diag index i within seg? offset i if 2i <2n-1 (always for i<n? 2i <=2n-2 yes)
            # diag limit sqrt(cap - (c[2i]-old^2))
            # But base at diag = seg[i] - 2*old*a[i] = c[2i]-2old^2? For limit formula off would be (cap - base)/(2a[i]) = (cap - cdiag +2old^2)/(2old). The correct diag limit root is sqrt(cap - (cdiag - old^2)). Need override.
            seg = c[i:i+n]
            # compute candidate limits only for a>0
            denom = 2.0 * a
            # To avoid divide by zero, use np.where
            # base = seg - 2*old*a
            # For places where denom>0 and offset !=i.
            # We can compute all, then set diag.
            base = seg - 2.0 * old * a
            with np.errstate(divide='ignore', invalid='ignore'):
                lim = (cap - base) / denom
            # where denom zero, lim=inf if base<=cap else 0 (but base should <=cap)
            lim = np.where(denom > 0, lim, np.inf)
            # diag offset i
            cd = c[2*i]
            diag_base = cd - old*old
            if diag_base < 0 and diag_base > -1e-12: diag_base=0.0
            # Solve x^2 + diag_base <= cap => x = sqrt(cap - diag_base) if cap>=diag_base
            disc = cap - diag_base
            if disc < 0: disc = 0.0
            lim[i] = math.sqrt(disc) if n>i else lim[i]  # i always < n
            # The minimum candidate may be less than 0 due numeric; clip to 0
            # limit = min(lim)
            limit = float(np.min(lim))
            if not np.isfinite(limit): limit = old # maybe no constraints; but shouldn't, diag limits it
            if limit < 0: limit=0.0
            # Allow only increase; but maybe current old > limit due numeric; keep old (or reduce to limit? For feasibility, reduce maybe)
            if limit > old + 1e-15 * max(1.0, old):
                new = limit * (1.0 - 1e-12)  # maintaining feasible
                delta = new - old
                # update c segment
                # c[i:i+n] += 2*delta*a + delta^2 at offset i (old a[i])
                # But a not updated yet; use old in a[i]
                c[i:i+n] += 2.0 * delta * a
                c[2*i] += delta*delta
                a[i] = new
                changed_any=True
            elif old > limit * (1 + 1e-9):
                # current infeasible due numerical; reduce to limit
                new = limit
                delta = new - old
                c[i:i+n] += 2.0 * delta * a
                c[2*i] += delta*delta
                a[i] = new
                changed_any=True
        total_s = a.sum()
        if not changed_any or abs(total_s - prev_s) <= tol*max(1.0, prev_s):
            prev_s = total_s
            break
        prev_s = total_s
    # Recompute c via FFT to eliminate drift
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    if c.max() > cap:
       a *= np.sqrt(cap / c.max())
    return a, float(a.sum())
```

- Precompute `inv_two_a = np.where(a>0, 1/(2*a), 0)`? But a changes as coordinates updated, so inv changes. We can update one entry after a[i] changes. This avoids reciprocal each i? ```

```
This still copies. We can use `np.subtract(c[i:i+n], 2*old*a, out=base)`, `np.divide(cap-base, denom, out=lim, where=denom>0)`. Then set lim[i]=diag, and `limit=lim.min()`. This reuses arrays, less allocation. Need be careful `denom` length n. `base` and `lim` length n.
Pseudo:
```
base = np.empty(n); lim=np.empty(n); denom=np.empty(n)
for pass:
  for i in order:
    old=a[i]
    np.subtract(c[i:i+n], 2.0*old*a, out=base)
    np.divide(cap - base, denom, out=lim, where=denom>0)
    # for denom<=0, lim default? np.divide leaves out unchanged; initialize lim.fill(np.inf) maybe costly; or handle denom zero later.
    # Better: set lim to inf where denom<=0 manually? We can do after: np.minimum(lim, ...)
```
Denom zeros: a_j=0; limit should be inf (no constraint) if base≤cap. We can avoid divide by zero by after divide, set `lim = np.where(denom > 0, lim, np.inf)`, but `np.where` allocates. ```

```

Scaling to cap1 function:
```
def _scale_cap(a, cap=1.0):
    if a.size==0: return a
    a=a.copy(); a[a<0]=0
    s=a.sum(); if s<1e-300: return a
    c=_fft_convolve(a); maxc=float(c.max())
    if maxc<=0: return a
    a *= math.sqrt(cap/maxc)
    # if maxc still >cap due fft, scale down slightly
    c=_fft_convolve(a); maxc=...; if maxc>cap*(1+1e-12): a*=math.sqrt(cap/maxc)*(1-1e-12)
    return a
```

```
def _coord_cap_optimize(a, cap, max_passes=50, tol=1e-10, random_order=True, seed=None, time_limit=None, start_time=0, verbose=False):
    ...
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    # if maxc > cap, scale down a and c by factor? If scale a by f, c*=f². If maxc>cap, f=sqrt(cap/maxc). Do a*=f; c*=f²? _fft_convolve again or scale c.
    ...
    # prepare arrays
    base=np.empty(n); lim=np.empty(n); tmp=np.empty(n); denom=np.empty(n)
    denom[:] = 2.0*a
    prev_s = a.sum()
    for p in range(max_passes):
       if time_limit... break
       # recompute c? Maybe at start pass to correct; but if we update c within pass, at start next pass c consistent. We can recompute every 5 passes or when drift.
       if p % 10 == 0:
          c = _fft_convolve(a)
          maxc=c.max()
          if maxc > cap*(1+1e-11):
              a *= math.sqrt(cap/maxc)
              c = _fft_convolve(a)
          denom[:] = 2.0*a
          prev_s maybe not?
       order = rng.permutation(n) if random_order and n<=5000 else np.arange(n)
       pass_start_sum = a.sum()
       for i in order:
          old=a[i]
          # if old maybe 0 and contribution? still can increase.
          np.subtract(c[i:i+n], 2.0*old*a, out=base)
          lim.fill(np.inf)
          np.divide(cap - base, denom, out=lim, where=denom > 0.0)
          # diag
          cd = c[2*i]
          diag_base = cd - old*old
          if diag_base < 0 and diag_base > -1e-9*cap: diag_base=0.0
          disc = cap - diag_base
          if disc < 0.0:
              # infeasible? reduce old to sqrt(cap-cd_base?) but diag_base = cd-old^2; if cd>cap, infeasible. Should have scaled.
              disc=0.0
          lim[i] = math.sqrt(disc)
          limit = float(np.min(lim, initial=0.0?)) # np.min不论 inf okay.
          if not finite: limit=old
          # If limit slightly below old due numeric, clamp old
          if limit < old and limit > old*(1-1e-9): limit=old
          if limit > old * (1.0 + 1e-12) + 1e-15:
              new = limit * (1.0 - 1e-12)
              delta = new - old
              np.multiply(2.0*delta, a, out=tmp)
              c[i:i+n] += tmp
              c[2*i] += delta*delta
              a[i] = new
              denom[i] = 2.0*new
              changed=True
          else:
              # if old > limit + ... (infeasible), reduce
              if old > limit * (1.0+1e-9) + 1e-15:
                   new=limit
                   delta=new-old
                   np.multiply(2.0*delta,a,out=tmp)
                   c[i:i+n]+=tmp
                   c[2*i]+=delta*delta
                   a[i]=new
                   denom[i]=2*new
       new_s = a.sum()
       if not changed or new_s <= pass_start_sum*(1+1e-12) + 1e-12:
           prev_s=new_s; break
       if new_s <= prev_s + tol*max(1.0, prev_s):
           # maybe stop if negligible
           # But there could be many tiny increases; set stop_after_two_little
           prev_s=new_s
           # break or continue? for search, break.
           # Use break to save time.
           break
       prev_s = new_s
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    maxc = float(c.max())
    if maxc > cap*(1+1e-10):
        a *= math.sqrt(cap/maxc)
        c = _fft_convolve(a); maxc=float(c.max())
    s=float(a.sum())
    return a, s, maxc
```

LP function top-level:
```
def _lp_best_response(f, cap=1.0):
    n=len(f)
    C = np.zeros((2*n-1,n))
    for j in range(n):
       C[j:j+n, j] = f
    c_obj = -np.ones(n)
    b_ub = cap*np.ones(2*n-1)
    bounds=[(0,None)]*n
    res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=C, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve':True})
    if res.status==0: return res.x
```
But f should be scaled? If f has max c≤cap? Cross constraints cap=1. If f is scaled to cap1, okay. The optimal g could have sum maybe >0. Then scale g to cap1 and coord max. This is one iteration. ```
A = np.vstack([C, C_rev])
b = cap*np.concatenate([ones, ones])
```
But C_rev for reverse f? We can build C for f and for f_rev. Or simpler, use f_smoothed = 0.5*(f+f_rev) so it has support both. LP with averaged f maybe enough. The best-response LP with f itself may bias to f's shape. Use several f: current best, reverse best, parent, P. We'll run a small number.

Now, code details for FFT convolution:
```
def _fft_convolve(a):
    n = len(a)
    size = 2*n -1
    fft_size = 1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, fft_size)
    # For nonnegative real a, c should real nonnegative; but numerical negative tiny.
    c = np.real(c[:size])
    # optional clean negative small: c[c<0 & c>-1e-12]=0? But negative could indicate issue; for score max, negative doesn't matter. For cap feasibility, negative c may make base >? We can clip negative to0 to avoid weird limits. Since true c≥0, do c = np.where(c<0,0,c). But small negative okay. We'll do c = np.maximum(c,0.0) maybe. However, clipping can hide if a has negative? We ensure a nonnegative. Good.
    return c
```
Clipping convolution to nonnegative may make c max slightly lower if true positive small negative? No.

`_interpolate_sequence(src, n_target)`:

if len(src)==0 return ones

return out
```

`_scale_to_cap(a, cap=1.0)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
a[a<0]=0
if a.size==0: return a
if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): a[~finite]=0
s=a.sum()
if s<=0: return a
c=_fft_convolve(a); maxc=float(c.max())
if maxc<=0: return a
a *= math.sqrt(cap/maxc)
# if a max >1000? cap1 prevents, but if cap large: clip? We use cap1. For safety clip to1000 then rescale? Maybe if cap>1000^2? not.
return a
```

spec = amp + 1j*rng.normal(0,1,n//2+1)/(1+freq)**1.5

```

```
def _try_start(a_start, upper, rng, deadline, passes_damp=None, passes_full=None, start_cap=1.0, scale_up=True, step_damp=0.5, step_full=1.0, random_order=True):
    a = np.asarray(a_start, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0 or a.sum() <= 0 and not (upper is not None and upper.max() >0):
        # If all zero but upper positive, coord can grow from zero; but need a vector length n. If a sum zero and upper has positive, coord okay. If all zero and no upper positive, return.
```
Actually if a all zero and upper has positive, `_coord_cap_optimize` can set coordinates from zero due diagonal limit. But if no upper finite positive and a zero, no growth. So if a.sum()==0 and (upper is None or upper.max()==0), return.

Scale start:
```
if a.sum() > 0:
    c = _fft_convolve(a); maxc = float(c.max())
    if maxc > 0:
       if maxc > start_cap*(1+1e-12):
           a *= math.sqrt(start_cap/maxc)
       elif scale_up and start_cap > 0 and maxc < start_cap:
           a *= math.sqrt(start_cap/maxc)
if upper is not None:
    up = np.asarray(upper, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.minimum(a, up)
```
If start_cap=0 and a small nonzero, no scale. If a all zero with upper positive, okay.

Implementation of `_coord_cap_optimize` with step and upper:
```
def _coord_cap_optimize(a, cap, upper=None, max_passes=50, step=1.0, random_order=True, rng=None, deadline=None):
    a = np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    upper = np.asarray(upper,dtype=np.float64) if upper is not None else None
    n=len(a)
    a[a<0]=0
    c=_fft_convolve(a)
    maxc=float(c.max())
    if maxc > cap*(1+1e-11):
        a *= math.sqrt(cap/maxc)
        c = _fft_convolve(a)
    if a.sum() <=0:
        return a,0.0,0.0
    denom = 2.0*a.copy()
    base = np.empty(n); lim=np.empty(n); tmp=np.empty(n)
    prev_s = float(a.sum())
    order = None
    for p in range(max_passes):
       if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       if p % 10 == 0:
          c = _fft_convolve(a)
          maxc=float(c.max())
          if maxc > cap*(1+1e-10):
             a *= math.sqrt(cap/maxc)
             c = _fft_convolve(a)
          denom[:] = 2.0*a
       if random_order and rng is not None and n <= 5000:
           order = rng.permutation(n)
       else:
           if order is None: order = np.arange(n)
           # maybe alternate reverse every other pass
           if random_order and n>5000: order = np.arange(n)
       pass_sum_start = float(a.sum())
       changed=False
       for ii, i in enumerate(order):
           if deadline is not None and (ii & 63)==0 and time.monotonic() > deadline: break
           old = a[i]
           if upper is not None and old > upper[i]*(1+1e-10):
               # enforce
               new=upper[i]
               delta=new-old
               if abs(delta)>0: update...
               continue
           # if old maybe 0 and upper==0, skip
           if upper is not None and upper[i] <= 1e-15:
               if old > 1e-15:
                   new=0.0; delta=-old; update; a[i]=0; denom[i]=0; changed=True
               continue
           np.subtract(c[i:i+n], 2.0*old*a, out=base)
           lim.fill(np.inf)
           np.divide(cap - base, denom, out=lim, where=denom > 0.0)
           # diag
           cd = c[2*i]
           diag_base = cd - old*old
           if diag_base < 0.0:
               if diag_base > -1e-9*cap: diag_base = 0.0
           disc = cap - diag_base
           if disc < 0.0: disc = 0.0
           lim[i] = math.sqrt(disc)
           # Replace possible nan? If cap-base negative and denom small? np.divide where denom>0 may produce negative; no nan unless inf? If denom zero skipped.
           # To remove nan from cap-base where base nan? a finite.
           # limit
           # We can't use np.min with initial? limit = float(np.min(lim)) handles inf.
           # If any nan, min nan. Ensure no nan: base=c-2old*a finite; cap-base finite. disc finite.
           limit = float(np.min(lim))
           if upper is not None:
               ul = float(upper[i])
               if ul < limit: limit = ul
           if not np.isfinite(limit):
               limit = old
           if limit < 0.0:
               if limit > -1e-9*cap: limit = 0.0
               else: limit = 0.0
           if limit > old:
               if step >= 1.0 or step is None:
                   new = limit * (1.0 - 1e-12)
               else:
                   new = old + step * (limit - old)
                   if new >= limit: new = limit * (1.0 - 1e-12)
               delta = new - old
               if delta > 0:
                   np.multiply(2.0*delta, a, out=tmp)
                   c[i:i+n] += tmp
                   c[2*i] += delta*delta
                   a[i] = new
                   denom[i] = 2.0*new
                   changed=True
           elif old > limit:
               if old <= limit*(1+1e-9)+1e-12:
                   # roundoff, ignore
                   pass
               else:
                   new = max(0.0, limit * (1.0 + 1e-12))
                   delta = new - old
                   if delta < 0:
                       np.multiply(2.0*delta, a, out=tmp)
                       c[i:i+n] += tmp
                       c[2*i] += delta*delta
                       a[i] = max(0.0, new)
                       denom[i] = max(0.0, 2.0*a[i])
                       changed=True
       new_s = float(a.sum())
       if not changed:
           prev_s = new_s
           break
       if new_s <= pass_sum_start + 1e-12:
           prev_s = new_s
           # break? If step<1, might have tiny changes; use small threshold
           break
       if step >=1.0:
           if new_s <= prev_s + 1e-9*max(1.0, prev_s):
               # Could still benefit? break to save
               prev_s = new_s
               # maybe one more? break
               break
       prev_s = new_s
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    maxc = float(c.max())
    if maxc > cap*(1+1e-9):
       a *= math.sqrt(cap/maxc)
       c = _fft_convolve(a); maxc=float(c.max())
    s = float(a.sum())
    return a, s, maxc
```
Issue: In `_coord_cap_optimize`, if `upper[i]` is inf, `np.isfinite(upper[i])` false but ul large; `if ul < limit` works with inf? inf < limit false. Good.

`np.subtract(c[i:i+n], 2.0*old*a, out=base)`: if `c[i:i+n]` length n, base length n. Good. But `2.0*old*a` creates temporary array length n. Could use `np.multiply(2.0*old, a, out=tmp)` then `np.subtract(c[i:i+n], tmp, out=base)` to avoid temporary? tmp reused, but we also need tmp for update. We can use another buffer `tmp2`. Simpler okay. For n=4096, temporary alloc per i may be heavy. Let's optimize:
- Allocate `lin = np.empty(n)` for old*a*2.
- In loop: `np.multiply(2.0*old, a, out=lin); np.subtract(c[i:i+n], lin, out=base)`.
- For update: `np.multiply(2.0*delta, a, out=lin); c[i:i+n] += lin; c[2*i] += delta*delta`. Reuse `lin` (no tmp). Good.
We need be careful if `lin` used and c slice updates in-place; fine.
Add `lin = np.empty(n)`.

When `old=0`, `lin` zeros; base=c. Limit off = cap/denom. Good.

Diagonal update if `2*i` may be outside c? 2i <=2n-2 for i<=n-1. Good.

If `a[i]=0` and `denom[i]=0`, update `a[i]` new; then `lin` multiply uses old a with a[i]=0; c diagonal update δ². Off updates for other l don't depend on a[i]? For coordinate i, update c[i:i+n] includes coefficient k=i+l. The change due to a_i is 2δ * a[l] + δ² if l=i. For l !=i, doesn't involve a[i]. Using `a` before update is correct. After update, a[i] changes; for future coords, c and a consistent.

If reducing an infeasible old (delta negative), `lin` uses old a values; formula c_new = c_old + 2δ a + δ² diagonal. For diag, if old reduced, c_diag change = new²-old² = 2δ old + δ², matches. Good.

Now, `_try_start` uses `_coord_cap_optimize` twice; after first, a may not be cap1 but feasible. Second with step1 may overfill but upper enforced.

Potential problem: The cap for coordinate optimization always 1.0. If the start has max c much less than 1 and no upper, coordinate max will scale up through increments; final max=1. Good. If start has max c >1, `_coord_cap_optimize` scales down. If scale_up False and start max<1, no initial scale; coordinate will still increase to1 because cap1. This is okay but might take many passes. For variant starts, use scale_up True.

Let's define a top-level function `_consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands, n_target=None)` returning `(best_a,best_score,cands)`. It checks if a not None, score finite, maybe score < best_score - 1e-12. If so update cands. This avoids nested.

```
def _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands, min_improve=1e-9):
    if a is None or not np.isfinite(score): return best_a,best_score,cands
    if score < best_score - 1e-12:
        best_score = float(score)
        best_a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        if len(cands) < 25:
            cands.append((best_score, best_a.copy()))
        elif best_score < cands[-1][0]:
            # replace worst? We can find max score in cands and replace
            worst_idx=0; worst=cands[0][0]
            for idx in range(1,len(cands)):
                if cands[idx][0] > worst:
                    worst=cands[idx][0]; worst_idx=idx
            cands[worst_idx]=(best_score, best_a.copy())
        print(f"  new best score={best_score:.8f} n={len(best_a)}")
    return best_a,best_score,cands
```
No lambda.

LP within `_search_n`: Only if n<=800 and time remains >60. `_lp_best_response` dense solve maybe seconds. We'll run at most 5 iterations. Need build C. Let's implement efficient C build:
```
def _lp_best_response(f, cap=1.0, use_mix=None):
    n=len(f)
    if use_mix is None: use_mix=True
    if use_mix:
       fm = 0.5*(f + f[::-1])
       fm = _scale_to_cap(fm, cap)
    else:
       fm = _scale_to_cap(f, cap)
    A = np.zeros((2*n-1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n):
       A[j:j+n, j] = fm
    c_obj = -np.ones(n)
    b_ub = np.full(2*n-1, cap)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)]*n
    try:
       res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
       if res.status == 0:
          g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
          g = np.maximum(g,0)
          return g
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
Building A via slice assignment with fm length n. If use_mix, constraints from mixed f. Good. For n=1000, A 999? Actually 1999x1000. Fine.
LP could return g with max self conv >1; _try_start will scale down.

if n==0: return a
a[0]=1.0
for i in range(n-1):
    a[i+1] = a[i] * (2.0*i + 1.0) / (2.0*i + 2.0)
return a
```

```

```

b = np.fft.irfft(X, n)
a = np.maximum(b, 0.0) + 0.05
return a
```

```
def _search_n(n, best_a, best_score, cands, rng, deadline, phase_seconds, do_endpoint=False, do_random=False, do_lp=False):
    phase_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + phase_seconds)
    # determine base_a
    base_a = best_a
    if base_a is None:
        base_a = np.ones(n)
    if len(base_a) != n:
        base_a = _interpolate_sequence(base_a, n)
    base_a = _scale_to_cap(base_a, 1.0)
    # refine base
    a, score = _try_start(base_a, None, rng, phase_deadline, passes_damp=20, passes_full=30, start_cap=1.0)
    best_a,best_score,cands = _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a,best_score,cands)
    # variant list
    variant_tags = [...]
    if n <= 700: pass_d=25; pass_f=20
    elif n <= 1600: pass_d=15; pass_f=12
    else: pass_d=8; pass_f=8
    for tag in variant_tags:
       if time.monotonic() > phase_deadline - 5: break
       v = _make_variant(n, tag, rng, base_a)
       if v is None: continue
       a, score = _try_start(v, None, rng, phase_deadline, passes_damp=pass_d, passes_full=pass_f, start_cap=1.0)
       best...=consider
    # endpoint sweep
    if do_endpoint and time.monotonic() < phase_deadline - 20:
       if n <=700: x_vals = np.array([0.0,0.1,0.25,0.4,0.6,0.8,1.0])
       elif n<=1600: x_vals=np.array([0.0,0.3,0.6,1.0])
       else: x_vals=np.array([0.0,0.5,1.0])
       if n <=700: ep_d=35; ep_f=20
       elif n<=1600: ep_d=18; ep_f=12
       else: ep_d=8; ep_f=6
       upper = np.full(n, np.inf)
       small = np.full(n, 1e-3)
       for x0 in x_vals:
           upper[0] = x0
           for x1 in x_vals:
               if time.monotonic() > phase_deadline - 5: break
               upper[-1] = x1
               a = np.minimum(small, upper)
               # if both x zero, small endpoints? upper=0 so endpoints zero.
               a, score = _try_start(a, upper, rng, phase_deadline, passes_damp=ep_d, passes_full=ep_f, start_cap=0.0, scale_up=False)
               best...=consider
           upper[-1] = np.inf
       upper[0]=np.inf
       # second endpoint refinement? Maybe if n<=700 and time remains:
       # We can run a few random endpoint caps from a distribution.
       if n <= 700 and time.monotonic() < phase_deadline - 30:
           for _ in range(10):
              x0 = float(rng.uniform(0,1)); x1=float(rng.uniform(0,1))
              upper[0]=x0; upper[-1]=x1
              a=np.minimum(small, upper)
              a,score=_try_start(...)
              consider
           upper[0]=np.inf; upper[-1]=np.inf
    # random perturbations
    if do_random and time.monotonic() < phase_deadline - 20:
       base = best_a if len(best_a)==n else _interpolate_sequence(best_a,n)
       base = _scale_to_cap(base,1.0)
       if n<=700: max_iter=80; rpass_d=15; rpass_f=15
       elif n<=1600: max_iter=30; ...
       else: max_iter=10; ...
       for it in range(max_iter):
           if time.monotonic() > phase_deadline - 5: break
           mode = rng.integers(0,3)
           if mode==0:
              sigma = 0.05 + 0.25*float(rng.random())
              a = base * np.exp(sigma * rng.normal(0,1,n))
              a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
           elif mode==1:
              w = 0.1 + 0.5*float(rng.random())
              v = _make_variant(n, 'rand', rng, base)
              if v is None: v = _p_sequence(n)
              a = (1-w)*base + w*v
              a = np.maximum(a,0)
           else:
              # local coordinate noise: sample subset
              a = base.copy()
              count = max(1, n//10)
              idx = rng.choice(n, count, replace=False)
              factor = np.exp(0.2*rng.normal(0,1,count))
              a[idx] *= factor
              a = np.maximum(a,0)
           a, score = _try_start(a, None, rng, phase_deadline, passes_damp=rpass_d, passes_full=rpass_f, start_cap=1.0)
           best...=consider
           base = best_a if len(best_a)==n else _interpolate_sequence(best_a,n)
           # maybe scale base to cap1 for next
           base = _scale_to_cap(base,1.0)
    # LP
    if do_lp and n <= 800 and time.monotonic() < phase_deadline - 60:
       f = base if base is not None and len==n else _p_sequence(n)
       f = _scale_to_cap(f,1.0)
       for it in range(5):
           if time.monotonic() > phase_deadline - 30: break
           g = _lp_best_response(f, 1.0, True)
           if g is None or g.sum()<=0: break
           a, score = _try_start(g, None, rng, phase_deadline, passes_damp=15, passes_full=12, start_cap=1.0)
           best...=consider
           if a is not None:
               f = _scale_to_cap(0.35*f + 0.65*a,1.0)
       # LP from P
       fP = _scale_to_cap(_p_sequence(n),1.0)
       for it in range(3): ...
    return best_a,best_score,cands
```

Detailed code:

```
"""docstring"""
import numpy as np
import time
import math
from scipy.optimize import linprog

def _fft_convolve(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2*n - 1
    fft_size = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, fft_size)
    c = c[:size].astype(np.float64, copy=False)
    # clean tiny negative
    if c.size:
        c[np.logical_and(c < 0.0, c > -1e-12)] = 0.0
        # For true nonnegative input, any negative is numerical; set all negative to 0? Could hide real? Input nonnegative true c nonnegative, so clip all negative.
        c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c
```
Clipping all negative to 0 okay.

`_score`:
```
def _score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return float('inf')
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-300: return float('inf')
    c=_fft_convolve(a)
    if c.size==0: return inf
    maxc=float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0*n*maxc/(s*s))
```

`_scale_to_cap`:
```
def _scale_to_cap(a, cap=1.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a[a<0] = 0.0
    if a.size==0 or a.sum() <= 0.0:
        return a
    if cap <= 0.0:
        return a
    c=_fft_convolve(a); maxc=float(np.max(c))
    if maxc <= 0.0:
        return a
    if maxc > cap:
        a *= math.sqrt(cap/maxc)
    elif maxc < cap:
        a *= math.sqrt(cap/maxc)
    # Safety clipping
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a
```
For cap=1, scale up/down. If maxc tiny, scale huge might make values >1000? If a sum small but maxc tiny, scaling to1 could make entries maybe >1? For a nonzero vector, scaling to cap1 ensures each a_i≤1 due c0=a0²≤1, so no >1. Good. If due numerical, np.clip.

    if not np.isfinite(score): return...
    if score >= best_score: return...
    arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    arr[~np.isfinite(arr)] = 0.0
    arr[arr<0] = 0.0
    best_score=float(score)
    best_a=arr
    if len(cands) < 30:
        cands.append((best_score, best_a.copy()))
    print(f"    ** new best score={best_score:.8f} n={best_a.size}")
    return best_a,best_score,cands
```

```
def _try_start(a_start, upper, rng, deadline, passes_damp=None, passes_full=None, start_cap=1.0, scale_up=True, step_damp=0.5, step_full=1.0, random_order=True):
    a=np.asarray(a_start,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size==0: return None, inf
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] =0; a[a<0]=0
    n=a.size
    up = None
    if upper is not None:
        up = np.asarray(upper,dtype=np.float64).copy()
        up[~np.isfinite(up)] = np.inf
        up[up<0] = 0
    if a.sum() <= 0.0 and (up is None or up.max() <= 0.0):
        # no way to grow
        return None, inf
    if a.sum() > 0.0:
       maxc = float(np.max(_fft_convolve(a)))
       if maxc > 0:
           if maxc > start_cap:
              a *= math.sqrt(start_cap/maxc) if start_cap>0 else 0.0
           elif scale_up and start_cap > 0:
              a *= math.sqrt(start_cap/maxc)
    if up is not None:
       a = np.minimum(a, up)
    # if all zero and up has positive, coord will grow from zero
    if time.monotonic() > deadline:
       score = _score(a)
       return (a if a.sum()>0 else None), score
    if passes_damp is None:
       if n <= 700: passes_damp=40
       elif n <= 1600: passes_damp=20
       else: passes_damp=8
    if passes_full is None:
       if n <= 700: passes_full=30
       elif n <= 1600: passes_full=15
       else: passes_full=8
    a, s, m = _coord_cap_optimize(a, 1.0, upper=up, max_passes=passes_damp, step=step_damp, random_order=random_order, rng=rng, deadline=deadline)
    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
       a, s, m = _coord_cap_optimize(a, 1.0, upper=up, max_passes=passes_full, step=step_full, random_order=random_order, rng=rng, deadline=deadline)
    if a.sum() <= 0:
       return None, inf
    score = _score(a)
    return a, score
```

But start small likely maxc<1. If a has maxc>1 and start_cap=0, we should scale down to1? Could do if maxc > 1: scale to1 regardless. So in `_try_start`, after maxc:

if maxc > 1.0:
   a *= sqrt(1.0/maxc)

```

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative coefficient sequences minimizing
J(a) = 2*n*max(convolve(a, a)) / sum(a)**2.

The main engine is coordinate-wise maximization of the total mass subject to
the constraint max a*a <= 1.  This is combined with:
  - endpoint / anchor upper-bound sweeps,
  - damped coordinate ascent from diffuse starts,
  - random perturbations and blends,
  - a small number of LP best-response polishing steps.
The code is intentionally time-boxed and returns the best sequence found.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math
from scipy.optimize import linprog


def _fft_convolve(a):
    """Full linear convolution a*a via FFT, clipped to nonnegative values."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    size = 2 * n - 1
    fft_size = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, fft_size)
    c = c[:size].astype(np.float64, copy=False)

    if c.size:
        c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c


def _score(a):
    """Fast score used during search."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")

    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-300:
        return float("inf")

    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")

    maxc = float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0 * n * maxc / (s * s))


def _scale_to_cap(a, cap=1.0):
    """Scale a sequence so max(conv(a, a)) is approximately cap (if cap > 0)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a[a < 0.0] = 0.0

    if a.size == 0 or a.sum() <= 0.0:
        return a

    if cap <= 0.0:
        # Still enforce absolute feasibility with cap=1 if needed.
        c = _fft_convolve(a)
        maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        if maxc > 1.0:
            a *= math.sqrt(1.0 / maxc)
        return a

    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if maxc <= 0.0:
        return a

    a *= math.sqrt(cap / maxc)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a


def _interpolate_sequence(src, n_target):
    """Linearly interpolate a sequence to a target length."""
    if src is None:
        return np.ones(n_target, dtype=np.float64)

    src = np.asarray(src, dtype=np.float64)
    if src.size == 0:
        return np.ones(n_target, dtype=np.float64)

    if src.size == n_target:
        out = src.copy()
    else:
        x_src = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, src.size)
        x_tgt = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_target)
        out = np.interp(x_tgt, x_src, src)

    out = np.asarray(out, dtype=np.float64)
    out[~np.isfinite(out)] = 0.0
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out


def _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands):
    """Update and store a better candidate."""
    if a is None:
        return best_a, best_score, cands
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return best_a, best_score, cands
    if score >= best_score:
        return best_a, best_score, cands

    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    arr[~np.isfinite(arr)] = 0.0
    arr[arr < 0.0] = 0.0

    best_a = arr
    best_score = float(score)

    if len(cands) < 30:
        cands.append((best_score, best_a.copy()))

    print(f"    ** new best score={best_score:.8f} n={best_a.size}")
    return best_a, best_score, cands


def _coord_cap_optimize(a, cap=1.0, upper=None, max_passes=50, step=1.0,
                        random_order=True, rng=None, deadline=None):
    """
    Coordinate ascent for:
        maximize sum(a)
        s.t. 0 <= a_i <= upper_i, max coeff of a*a <= cap.

    The update for coordinate i is exact for the current other coordinates:
    it chooses the largest x (or damped fraction toward it) such that all
    convolution coefficients remain feasible.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a[a < 0.0] = 0.0
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a, 0.0, 0.0

    if upper is not None:
        upper = np.asarray(upper, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        upper[~np.isfinite(upper)] = np.inf
        upper[upper < 0.0] = 0.0
    else:
        upper = None

    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if maxc > cap * (1.0 + 1e-11):
        a *= math.sqrt(cap / maxc)
        c = _fft_convolve(a)
        maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0

    if a.sum() <= 0.0:
        return a, 0.0, 0.0

    denom = 2.0 * a.copy()
    base = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    lin = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    lim = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)

    order_fwd = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    order_bwd = order_fwd[::-1]

    for p in range(max_passes):
        if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

        # Periodically recompute convolution to remove numerical drift.
        if p % 10 == 0:
            c = _fft_convolve(a)
            maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
            if maxc > cap * (1.0 + 1e-10):
                scale = math.sqrt(cap / maxc)
                a *= scale
                c = _fft_convolve(a)
            denom[:] = 2.0 * a

        if random_order and rng is not None and n <= 6000:
            order = rng.permutation(n)
        else:
            order = order_bwd if (p & 1) else order_fwd

        pass_sum_start = float(a.sum())
        changed = False
        time_break = False

        for ii, i in enumerate(order):
            if deadline is not None and (ii & 63) == 0 and time.monotonic() > deadline:
                time_break = True
                break

            old = a[i]

            # Enforce upper bound if violated.
            if upper is not None:
                ul = upper[i]
                if ul <= 1e-15:
                    if old > 1e-15:
                        new = 0.0
                        delta = new - old
                        np.multiply(2.0 * delta, a, out=lin)
                        c[i:i + n] += lin
                        c[2 * i] += delta * delta
                        a[i] = 0.0
                        denom[i] = 0.0
                        changed = True
                    continue
                if old > ul * (1.0 + 1e-10):
                    new = ul
                    delta = new - old
                    np.multiply(2.0 * delta, a, out=lin)
                    c[i:i + n] += lin
                    c[2 * i] += delta * delta
                    a[i] = new
                    denom[i] = 2.0 * new
                    changed = True
                    continue

            # Compute feasible limit for coordinate i.
            np.multiply(2.0 * old, a, out=lin)
            np.subtract(c[i:i + n], lin, out=base)   # base = c - 2*old*a
            np.subtract(cap, base, out=base)         # base = cap - (c - 2*old*a)

            lim.fill(np.inf)
            np.divide(base, denom, out=lim, where=denom > 0.0)

            # Diagonal constraint k = 2*i is quadratic: (x)^2 <= cap - base_diag.
            cd = c[2 * i]
            diag_base = cd - old * old
            if diag_base < 0.0 and diag_base > -1e-9 * cap:
                diag_base = 0.0
            disc = cap - diag_base
            if disc < 0.0:
                disc = 0.0
            lim[i] = math.sqrt(disc)

            limit = float(np.min(lim))

            if upper is not None:
                ul = upper[i]
                if ul < limit:
                    limit = ul

            if not np.isfinite(limit):
                limit = old
            if limit < 0.0:
                limit = 0.0

            if limit > old:
                if step >= 1.0:
                    new = limit * (1.0 - 1e-12)
                else:
                    new = old + step * (limit - old)
                    if new >= limit:
                        new = limit * (1.0 - 1e-12)

                delta = new - old
                if delta > 0.0:
                    np.multiply(2.0 * delta, a, out=lin)
                    c[i:i + n] += lin
                    c[2 * i] += delta * delta
                    a[i] = new
                    denom[i] = 2.0 * new
                    changed = True

            elif old > limit:
                if old > limit * (1.0 + 1e-9) + 1e-15:
                    new = max(0.0, limit * (1.0 + 1e-12))
                    delta = new - old
                    np.multiply(2.0 * delta, a, out=lin)
                    c[i:i + n] += lin
                    c[2 * i] += delta * delta
                    a[i] = max(0.0, new)
                    denom[i] = max(0.0, 2.0 * a[i])
                    changed = True

        if time_break:
            break

        new_s = float(a.sum())
        if not changed:
            break

        if new_s <= pass_sum_start + 1e-10 * max(1.0, pass_sum_start):
            break

    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if maxc > cap * (1.0 + 1e-9):
        a *= math.sqrt(cap / maxc)
        c = _fft_convolve(a)
        maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0

    return a, float(a.sum()), maxc


def _p_sequence(n):
    """
    P_i = binom(2i, i) / 4^i.
    Its infinite truncation has convolution coefficients equal to 1 for the
    first half, giving the classical pi/2 construction.
    """
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0:
        return a

    a[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(n - 1):
        a[i + 1] = a[i] * (2.0 * i + 1.0) / (2.0 * i + 2.0)
    return a


def _power_seq(n, alpha, reverse=False):
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
    a = idx ** (-alpha)
    if reverse:
        a = a[::-1]
    return a


def _two_gauss_start(n, c1, c2, s1, s2, w1, w2):
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = (
        w1 * np.exp(-((t - c1) ** 2) / (2.0 * s1 * s1)) +
        w2 * np.exp(-((t - c2) ** 2) / (2.0 * s2 * s2))
    )
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0) + 1e-6
    return a


def _smooth_random_start(n, rng):
    x = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
    X = np.fft.rfft(x)
    fr = 1.0 + np.arange(len(X), dtype=np.float64) ** 1.0
    X = X / fr
    b = np.fft.irfft(X, n)
    a = np.maximum(b, 0.0) + 0.05
    return a


def _make_variant(n, tag, rng, base_a):
    """Generate one heuristic start sequence of length n."""
    if tag == "p":
        return _p_sequence(n)
    if tag == "p_rev":
        return _p_sequence(n)[::-1]
    if tag == "uni":
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if tag == "pw03":
        return _power_seq(n, 0.3)
    if tag == "pw06":
        return _power_seq(n, 0.6)
    if tag == "pw10":
        return _power_seq(n, 1.0)
    if tag == "pw13":
        return _power_seq(n, 1.3)
    if tag == "pw06r":
        return _power_seq(n, 0.6, reverse=True)
    if tag == "pw10r":
        return _power_seq(n, 1.0, reverse=True)

    if tag == "g1":
        return _two_gauss_start(n, 0.25, 0.75, 0.08, 0.10, 1.0, 1.0)
    if tag == "g2":
        return _two_gauss_start(n, 0.30, 0.70, 0.05, 0.08, 1.0, 0.7)
    if tag == "g3":
        return _two_gauss_start(n, 0.15, 0.85, 0.12, 0.12, 1.0, 1.0)

    p = _p_sequence(n)
    pr = p[::-1]

    if tag == "mix1":
        return 0.1 * p + 0.9 * pr
    if tag == "mix3":
        return 0.3 * p + 0.7 * pr
    if tag == "mix5":
        return 0.5 * p + 0.5 * pr
    if tag == "mix7":
        return 0.7 * p + 0.3 * pr
    if tag == "mix9":
        return 0.9 * p + 0.1 * pr

    if tag == "rand1" or tag == "rand2":
        return _smooth_random_start(n, rng)

    if tag == "base":
        if base_a is None:
            return None
        if len(base_a) == n:
            return base_a.copy()
        return _interpolate_sequence(base_a, n)

    return None


def _lp_best_response(f, cap=1.0, use_mix=True):
    """
    Solve one linear best-response step:
        maximize sum(g)
        s.t. f*g <= cap, g >= 0.

    If use_mix is True, use a symmetric/causal-blended version of f, which
    often produces more balanced candidates.
    """
    n = f.size if f is not None else 0
    if n == 0:
        return None

    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    if use_mix:
        fm = 0.5 * (f + f[::-1])
    else:
        fm = f.copy()

    fm = _scale_to_cap(fm, cap)
    if fm.sum() <= 0.0:
        return None

    A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j + n, j] = fm

    c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.full(2 * n - 1, cap, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n

    try:
        res = linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"presolve": True}
        )
        if res.status == 0:
            g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
            g[~np.isfinite(g)] = 0.0
            return g
    except Exception:
        return None

    return None


def _try_start(a_start, upper, rng, deadline,
               passes_damp=None, passes_full=None,
               start_cap=1.0, scale_up=True,
               step_damp=0.5, step_full=1.0,
               random_order=True):
    """Prepare a start, run damped then full coordinate cap optimization."""
    a = np.asarray(a_start, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return None, float("inf")

    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a[a < 0.0] = 0.0

    n = a.size
    up = None
    if upper is not None:
        up = np.asarray(upper, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        up[~np.isfinite(up)] = np.inf
        up[up < 0.0] = 0.0

    if a.sum() <= 0.0 and (up is None or up.max() <= 0.0):
        return None, float("inf")

    if a.sum() > 0.0:
        c = _fft_convolve(a)
        maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        if maxc > 0.0:
            if maxc > 1.0:
                a *= math.sqrt(1.0 / maxc)
            elif start_cap > 0.0:
                if maxc > start_cap:
                    a *= math.sqrt(start_cap / maxc)
                elif scale_up:
                    a *= math.sqrt(start_cap / maxc)

    if up is not None:
        a = np.minimum(a, up)

    if time.monotonic() > deadline:
        score = _score(a)
        return (a if a.sum() > 0.0 else None), score

    if passes_damp is None:
        if n <= 700:
            passes_damp = 45
        elif n <= 1600:
            passes_damp = 22
        else:
            passes_damp = 10

    if passes_full is None:
        if n <= 700:
            passes_full = 30
        elif n <= 1600:
            passes_full = 16
        else:
            passes_full = 8

    a, _, _ = _coord_cap_optimize(
        a, 1.0, upper=up,
        max_passes=passes_damp,
        step=step_damp,
        random_order=random_order,
        rng=rng,
        deadline=deadline
    )

    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
        a, _, _ = _coord_cap_optimize(
            a, 1.0, upper=up,
            max_passes=passes_full,
            step=step_full,
            random_order=random_order,
            rng=rng,
            deadline=deadline
        )

    if a.sum() <= 0.0:
        return None, float("inf")

    score = _score(a)
    return a, score


def _search_n(n, best_a, best_score, cands, rng, deadline,
              phase_seconds, do_endpoint=True, do_random=True, do_lp=True):
    """Run a time-boxed search at a fixed sequence length n."""
    phase_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + phase_seconds)

    # Initial base: global best if available, otherwise P.
    if best_a is None:
        base_a = _p_sequence(n)
    elif best_a.size == n:
        base_a = best_a.copy()
    else:
        base_a = _interpolate_sequence(best_a, n)
    base_a = _scale_to_cap(base_a, 1.0)

    # Refine the incoming base.
    a, score = _try_start(
        base_a, None, rng, phase_deadline,
        passes_damp=25, passes_full=30,
        start_cap=1.0, scale_up=True,
        step_damp=0.5, step_full=1.0,
        random_order=(n <= 2500)
    )
    best_a, best_score, cands = _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands)

    # Deterministic heuristic starts.
    if n <= 900:
        tags = [
            "p", "p_rev", "uni",
            "pw03", "pw06", "pw10", "pw13",
            "pw06r", "pw10r",
            "g1", "g2", "g3",
            "mix1", "mix3", "mix5", "mix7", "mix9",
            "rand1", "rand2", "base"
        ]
        pass_d = 22
        pass_f = 18
    else:
        tags = [
            "p", "p_rev", "uni",
            "pw06", "pw06r",
            "g1",
            "mix3", "mix7",
            "rand1", "base"
        ]
        pass_d = 12
        pass_f = 10

    for tag in tags:
        if time.monotonic() > phase_deadline - 5.0:
            break
        v = _make_variant(n, tag, rng, base_a)
        if v is None:
            continue
        a, score = _try_start(
            v, None, rng, phase_deadline,
            passes_damp=pass_d, passes_full=pass_f,
            start_cap=1.0, scale_up=True,
            step_damp=0.5, step_full=1.0,
            random_order=(n <= 2500)
        )
        best_a, best_score, cands = _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands)

    # Diffuse annealed start.
    if time.monotonic() < phase_deadline - 20.0:
        if n <= 900:
            ad = 140
            af = 50
            sd = 0.10
        elif n <= 2000:
            ad = 55
            af = 18
            sd = 0.15
        else:
            ad = 20
            af = 8
            sd = 0.20

        a_small = np.full(n, 1e-3, dtype=np.float64)
        a, score = _try_start(
            a_small, None, rng, phase_deadline,
            passes_damp=ad, passes_full=af,
            start_cap=0.0, scale_up=False,
            step_damp=sd, step_full=1.0,
            random_order=True
        )
        best_a, best_score, cands = _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands)

    # Endpoint / anchor cap sweeps.
    if do_endpoint and n >= 2 and time.monotonic() < phase_deadline - 30.0:
        if n <= 900:
            x_vals = np.array([0.0, 0.1, 0.25, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1.0], dtype=np.float64)
            ep_d = 35
            ep_f = 20
            ep_sd = 0.20
        elif n <= 2000:
            x_vals = np.array([0.0, 0.3, 0.6, 1.0], dtype=np.float64)
            ep_d = 16
            ep_f = 12
            ep_sd = 0.25
        else:
            x_vals = np.array([0.0, 0.5, 1.0], dtype=np.float64)
            ep_d = 8
            ep_f = 6
            ep_sd = 0.30

        upper = np.full(n, np.inf, dtype=np.float64)
        small = np.full(n, 1e-3, dtype=np.float64)

        for x0 in x_vals:
            upper[0] = x0
            for x1 in x_vals:
                if time.monotonic() > phase_deadline - 5.0:
                    break
                upper[-1] = x1
                a = np.minimum(small, upper)
                a, score = _try_start(
                    a, upper, rng, phase_deadline,
                    passes_damp=ep_d, passes_full=ep_f,
                    start_cap=0.0, scale_up=False,
                    step_damp=ep_sd, step_full=1.0,
                    random_order=True
                )
                best_a, best_score, cands = _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands)
            upper[-1] = np.inf
        upper[0] = np.inf

        # A few random endpoint caps.
        if n <= 1200 and time.monotonic() < phase_deadline - 25.0:
            for _ in range(8):
                if time.monotonic() > phase_deadline - 5.0:
                    break
                x0 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
                x1 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
                upper[0] = x0
                upper[-1] = x1
                a = np.minimum(small, upper)
                a, score = _try_start(
                    a, upper, rng, phase_deadline,
                    passes_damp=ep_d, passes_full=ep_f,
                    start_cap=0.0, scale_up=False,
                    step_damp=ep_sd, step_full=1.0,
                    random_order=True
                )
                best_a, best_score, cands = _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands)
            upper[0] = np.inf
            upper[-1] = np.inf

    # Random local search around the current best.
    if do_random and time.monotonic() < phase_deadline - 20.0:
        if best_a is None:
            base = _p_sequence(n)
        elif best_a.size == n:
            base = best_a.copy()
        else:
            base = _interpolate_sequence(best_a, n)
        base = _scale_to_cap(base, 1.0)

        if n <= 900:
            max_iter = 90
            rp_d = 14
            rp_f = 14
        elif n <= 2000:
            max_iter = 35
            rp_d = 8
            rp_f = 8
        else:
            max_iter = 12
            rp_d = 5
            rp_f = 5

        for _ in range(max_iter):
            if time.monotonic() > phase_deadline - 5.0:
                break

            if base is None or base.size != n:
                base = _p_sequence(n) if base is None else _interpolate_sequence(base, n)
                base = _scale_to_cap(base, 1.0)

            mode = int(rng.integers(0, 3))
            if mode == 0:
                sigma = 0.05 + 0.25 * float(rng.random())
                a = base * np.exp(sigma * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n))
                a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
            elif mode == 1:
                w = 0.1 + 0.5 * float(rng.random())
                v = _smooth_random_start(n, rng)
                a = (1.0 - w) * base + w * v
                a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
            else:
                a = base.copy()
                count = max(1, n // 10)
                idx = rng.choice(n, count, replace=False)
                factor = np.exp(0.2 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, count))
                a[idx] *= factor
                a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)

            a, score = _try_start(
                a, None, rng, phase_deadline,
                passes_damp=rp_d, passes_full=rp_f,
                start_cap=1.0, scale_up=True,
                step_damp=0.35, step_full=1.0,
                random_order=(n <= 2500)
            )
            best_a, best_score, cands = _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands)

            if best_a is not None:
                if best_a.size == n:
                    base = best_a.copy()
                else:
                    base = _interpolate_sequence(best_a, n)
                base = _scale_to_cap(base, 1.0)

    # LP best-response polishing. Keep this small because dense LPs can be slow.
    if do_lp and n <= 500 and time.monotonic() < phase_deadline - 60.0:
        if best_a is None:
            f = _p_sequence(n)
        elif best_a.size == n:
            f = best_a.copy()
        else:
            f = _interpolate_sequence(best_a, n)
        f = _scale_to_cap(f, 1.0)

        lp_deadline = min(phase_deadline - 30.0, time.monotonic() + 90.0)

        for _ in range(4):
            if time.monotonic() > lp_deadline:
                break
            g = _lp_best_response(f, 1.0, use_mix=True)
            if g is None or g.sum() <= 0.0:
                break
            a, score = _try_start(
                g, None, rng, lp_deadline,
                passes_damp=12, passes_full=10,
                start_cap=1.0, scale_up=True,
                step_damp=0.4, step_full=1.0,
                random_order=False
            )
            best_a, best_score, cands = _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands)
            if a is not None:
                f = _scale_to_cap(0.35 * f + 0.65 * a, 1.0)

        # Also try LP from P.
        if time.monotonic() < lp_deadline - 10.0:
            fP = _scale_to_cap(_p_sequence(n), 1.0)
            for _ in range(3):
                if time.monotonic() > lp_deadline:
                    break
                g = _lp_best_response(fP, 1.0, use_mix=False)
                if g is None or g.sum() <= 0.0:
                    break
                a, score = _try_start(
                    g, None, rng, lp_deadline,
                    passes_damp=12, passes_full=10,
                    start_cap=1.0, scale_up=True,
                    step_damp=0.4, step_full=1.0,
                    random_order=False
                )
                best_a, best_score, cands = _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands)
                if a is not None:
                    fP = _scale_to_cap(0.35 * fP + 0.65 * a, 1.0)

    return best_a, best_score, cands


def propose_candidate():
    """
    Time-boxed multi-length search.
    Uses the parent sequence when available, then searches at lengths 500,
    1000, 2000, 4096 (subject to time), and finally verifies candidates with
    the supplied evaluator.
    """
    deadline = time.monotonic() + 2340.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(314159)

    best_a = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    cands = []

    # Use the supplied parent construction if present.
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if p.ndim != 1 or p.size == 0:
            raise ValueError("bad parent shape")
        p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
        p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if p.sum() > 0.0:
            p = _scale_to_cap(p, 1.0)
            p_score = _score(p)
            best_a, best_score, cands = _consider_candidate(p, p_score, best_a, best_score, cands)
            print(f"Parent: n={p.size}, score={p_score:.8f}")
    except Exception:
        pass

    if best_a is None:
        best_a = _scale_to_cap(_p_sequence(500), 1.0)
        best_score = _score(best_a)
        cands.append((best_score, best_a.copy()))

    print(f"Initial best: score={best_score:.8f}, n={best_a.size}")

    # Decide search lengths. Include parent length if reasonable.
    first_n = int(best_a.size) if 200 <= best_a.size <= 1200 else 500
    n_list = [first_n, 500, 1000, 2000, 4096]

    seen = set()
    uniq = []
    for x in n_list:
        x = int(x)
        if x < 2 or x in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(x)
        uniq.append(x)
    n_list = uniq

    phase_seconds = {
        500: 820,
        1000: 620,
        2000: 430,
        4096: 270,
    }

    for n in n_list:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 170.0:
            break

        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic() - 170.0
        sec = min(int(phase_seconds.get(n, 220)), int(remaining))
        if sec <= 40:
            break

        do_endpoint = (n <= 1200)
        do_random = True
        do_lp = (n <= 500)

        print(f"\n=== Search length n={n}, budget={sec}s ===")
        best_a, best_score, cands = _search_n(
            n, best_a, best_score, cands, rng, deadline, sec,
            do_endpoint=do_endpoint, do_random=do_random, do_lp=do_lp
        )

    # Final micro-polish around the current best.
    if best_a is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 80.0:
        n_best = best_a.size
        base = _scale_to_cap(best_a, 1.0)

        polish_deadline = deadline - 30.0
        for _ in range(8):
            if time.monotonic() > polish_deadline:
                break
            base = _interpolate_sequence(base, n_best)
            base = _scale_to_cap(base, 1.0)

            a = base * np.exp(0.05 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n_best))
            a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)

            if n_best <= 900:
                pd, pf, sd = 35, 35, 0.25
            elif n_best <= 2000:
                pd, pf, sd = 16, 16, 0.30
            else:
                pd, pf, sd = 8, 8, 0.35

            a, score = _try_start(
                a, None, rng, polish_deadline,
                passes_damp=pd, passes_full=pf,
                start_cap=1.0, scale_up=True,
                step_damp=sd, step_full=1.0,
                random_order=(n_best <= 2500)
            )
            best_a, best_score, cands = _consider_candidate(a, score, best_a, best_score, cands)

            if a is not None:
                base = _scale_to_cap(a, 1.0)

    # Final safety fallback.
    if best_a is None:
        best_a = _scale_to_cap(_p_sequence(500), 1.0)

    # Select the best candidate by the actual evaluator, if available.
    final_candidates = []
    final_candidates.append(best_a)
    for _, arr in cands:
        final_candidates.append(arr)

    actual_best_a = None
    actual_best_val = float("inf")
    checked = 0

    for arr in final_candidates:
        if checked >= 12:
            break
        if arr is None:
            continue

        out = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        out[~np.isfinite(out)] = 0.0
        out[out < 0.0] = 0.0

        if out.size == 0 or out.sum() <= 1e-12:
            continue

        if out.max() > 1000.0:
            out *= (1000.0 / out.max())

        # Avoid the evaluator's sum<0.01 guard.
        if out.sum() < 0.1:
            out *= (1.0 / out.sum())
            if out.max() > 1000.0:
                out *= (1000.0 / out.max())

        try:
            val = float(evaluate_sequence(out.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            val = float("inf")

        if np.isfinite(val) and val < actual_best_val:
            actual_best_val = val
            actual_best_a = out
        checked += 1

    if actual_best_a is None:
        actual_best_a = best_a.copy()

    actual_best_a = np.asarray(actual_best_a, dtype=np.float64)
    actual_best_a[~np.isfinite(actual_best_a)] = 0.0
    actual_best_a[actual_best_a < 0.0] = 0.0
    actual_best_a = np.minimum(actual_best_a, 1000.0)

    result = [float(x) for x in actual_best_a]
    return result
```
